```text
These viscous solutions were degassed under vacuum, cast into a Teflon mold, and then dried in a vacuum oven at 65 °C for 48 h to obtain the PEO/LiFSI, PTMG-HDI/LiFSI, and PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI electrolytes.
```

Original evidence — main · Results / Electrochemical characterization of the SPEs, paragraph 13 · chunk src\-85520225f19b69cfbf86

```text
PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI (2.4 × 10⁻⁴ S cm⁻¹) exhibited a significantly higher ionic conductivity than PTMG-HDI/LiFSI (6.5 × 10⁻⁵ S cm⁻¹) and PEO/LiFSI (1.2 × 10⁻⁵ S cm⁻¹) at 30 °C, indicating that the strong interfacial self-healing and adhesion significantly decreased the interfacial resistance of the SS@SPE, and the amorphous polymer skeleton enabled fast Li⁺ transport.
```

ionic conductivity: 2\.4 × 10⁻⁴ S cm⁻¹

Specimen: PTMG\-HDI\-BHDS/LiFSI electrolyte

Original evidence — main · Results / Electrochemical characterization of the SPEs, paragraph 13 · chunk src\-85520225f19b69cfbf86

```text
PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI (2.4 × 10⁻⁴ S cm⁻¹) exhibited a significantly higher ionic conductivity than PTMG-HDI/LiFSI (6.5 × 10⁻⁵ S cm⁻¹) and PEO/LiFSI (1.2 × 10⁻⁵ S cm⁻¹) at 30 °C, indicating that the strong interfacial self-healing and adhesion significantly decreased the interfacial resistance of the SS@SPE, and the amorphous polymer skeleton enabled fast Li⁺ transport.
```

ionic conductivity: 1\.2 × 10⁻⁵ S cm⁻¹

Original evidence — main · Results / Electrochemical characterization of the SPEs, paragraph 13 · chunk src\-85520225f19b69cfbf86

```text
PTMG-HDI-BHDS/LiFSI (2.4 × 10⁻⁴ S cm⁻¹) exhibited a significantly higher ionic conductivity than PTMG-HDI/LiFSI (6.5 × 10⁻⁵ S cm⁻¹) and PEO/LiFSI (1.2 × 10⁻⁵ S cm⁻¹) at 30 °C, indicating that the strong interfacial self-healing and adhesion significantly decreased the interfacial resistance of the SS@SPE, and the amorphous polymer skeleton enabled fast Li⁺ transport.
```


Then, by adding N,N’\-4,4′\-diphenylmethanebismaleimide \(BMI\) to the N\-methylpyrrolidone \(NMP\) solution containing the precursor polymer and lithium bis\(oxalato\)borate \(LiBOB\), we created a crosslinked mechanically robust polymer matrix in situ to provide lithium\-ion transport pathways and thermal response self\-healing ability after filming and drying\.


Original evidence — main · 3\. Results / 3\.1\. Synthesis and Characterization of SHP, paragraph 13 · chunk src\-e9ce13d1849f7d925147

```text
Then, by adding N,N’-4,4′-diphenylmethanebismaleimide (BMI) to the N-methylpyrrolidone (NMP) solution containing the precursor polymer and lithium bis(oxalato)borate (LiBOB), we created a crosslinked mechanically robust polymer matrix in situ to provide lithium-ion transport pathways and thermal response self-healing ability after filming and drying.
```

Specimen: All the SHP electrolyte membranes used in electrochemical performance tests

Original evidence — main · 3\. Results / 3\.3\. Electrochemical Performances of SHP Electrolyte Membranes, paragraph 23 · chunk src\-84988e5beedccc653dff

```text
Figure 6a presents the plots of the ionic conductivities of SHP electrolyte membranes and PE films, and the Arrhenius plot for ionic conductivity as a function of temperature of the SHP electrolyte membranes has shown in Figure S2 (In details, the ionic conductivities of SHPEM at different temperature of 25 °C, 35 °C, 45 °C, 55 °C, 65 °C, 70 °C, 85 °C and 90 °C are 1.69 × 10⁻⁴, 2.69 × 10⁻⁴, 4.17 × 10⁻⁴, 6.61 × 10⁻⁴, 9.33 × 10⁻⁴, 1.32 × 10⁻³, 1.86 × 10⁻³ and 2.34 × 10⁻³ S·cm⁻¹, respectively).
```

Original evidence — main · Methods / Polymer synthesis, paragraph 29 · chunk src\-abdf1ced28f9b1e9fb83

```text
HO–PDMS–OH (23.0 g, 5.0 mmol) in a dried glass vessel equipped with a mechanical stirrer was heated in an oil bath at 100 °C under vacuum (<133 Pa) for 1 h to remove any moisture, and then cooled to 70 °C. IPDI (2.44 g, 11 mmol) and DBTDL (0.08 g) were dissolved in DMAc (20 mL), dropwise added into the vessel, and stirred for 3 h under a N₂ atmosphere. After the synthesis of the pre-polymer, 4,4^{′}-dithiodianiline (0.6 g, 2.5 mmol) and 4, 4^{′}-bis(hydroxymethyl)-2,2^{′}-bipyridine (0.465 g, 2.5 mmol) in 6 mL of dry DMAc were added to the pre-polymer solution. After stirring at 70 °C for another 3 h, the solution was added by methanol (3 mL), and further stirred for 30 min to ensure the consumption enough of isocyanate groups.
```

P3 \(1\.00 g\) was dissolved in 6 mL of CH₂Cl₂\. Subsequently, the solution was ultrasonicated to remove bubbles and poured into a PTFE dish \(diameter 3\.5 cm\) that was covered with a glass petri dish, slowly evaporated at room temperature overnight\. The resulting film was turned over and dried again for 8 h\. Afterward, the film was dried at 65 °C in a vacuum oven for 12 h\. After drying, the film was cut into small pieces for further testing\.

Specimen: P3 film

Original evidence — main · Methods / Film preparation for self\-healing and mechanical property testing, paragraph 30 · chunk src\-3c26b5356cd78c6d5409

```text
P3 (1.00 g) was dissolved in 6 mL of CH₂Cl₂. Subsequently, the solution was ultrasonicated to remove bubbles and poured into a PTFE dish (diameter 3.5 cm) that was covered with a glass petri dish, slowly evaporated at room temperature overnight. The resulting film was turned over and dried again for 8 h. Afterward, the film was dried at 65 °C in a vacuum oven for 12 h. After drying, the film was cut into small pieces for further testing. The same procedure was used to prepare films based on other ratios (PXs).
```

Sample-link evidence — main · Methods / Film preparation for self\-healing and mechanical property testing, paragraph 30 · chunk src\-3c26b5356cd78c6d5409

```text
P3 (1.00 g) was dissolved in 6 mL of CH₂Cl₂. Subsequently, the solution was ultrasonicated to remove bubbles and poured into a PTFE dish (diameter 3.5 cm) that was covered with a glass petri dish, slowly evaporated at room temperature overnight. The resulting film was turned over and dried again for 8 h. Afterward, the film was dried at 65 °C in a vacuum oven for 12 h. After drying, the film was cut into small pieces for further testing. The same procedure was used to prepare films based on other ratios (PXs).
```

P3 film

Original evidence — main · Methods / Film preparation for self\-healing and mechanical property testing, paragraph 30 · chunk src\-3c26b5356cd78c6d5409

```text
P3 (1.00 g) was dissolved in 6 mL of CH₂Cl₂. Subsequently, the solution was ultrasonicated to remove bubbles and poured into a PTFE dish (diameter 3.5 cm) that was covered with a glass petri dish, slowly evaporated at room temperature overnight. The resulting film was turned over and dried again for 8 h. Afterward, the film was dried at 65 °C in a vacuum oven for 12 h. After drying, the film was cut into small pieces for further testing. The same procedure was used to prepare films based on other ratios (PXs).
```

Original evidence — main · Results / Rheological and mechanical properties, paragraph 15 · chunk src\-64b8cc91509ada3d567a

```text
In e-skin or e-wear applications, the commonly used substrates usually suffer from instability, low toughness, or high notch sensitivity, which significantly reduces their service life. For example, typical PDMS (Sylgard 184) can be broken at 200% strain, polyurethane at 700% strain, and styrene butadiene styrene block copolymer (saturated) breaks at 280% strain⁴.
```
